    So `f2f3` is a direct attack on the Queen!
    This forces the Queen to move.
    If the Queen moves, it may no longer attack the Knight on b1.

    So, we have:
    1.  `e3b6` - Capture Bishop, but lose Knight at b1.
    2.  `f2f3` - Attack Queen, save Knight (by forcing the Queen to move).
    3.  `b1d2` - Save Knight, but don't capture anything.

    Which is better?
    `f2f3` seems very strong because it gains a tempo by attacking the Queen.

    If White plays `f2f3`, the Queen on e4 must move.

    She could potentially capture something or move to a better square.
    Can she capture the pawn on f3?
    No, pawns capture diagonally. The Queen can capture the pawn on f3.

    If Black plays `Qxf3`, they've captured a pawn.
    But now the Queen is on f3.
    Is the Queen on f3 safe?
    White has a rook on f1.
    If the Queen is on f3, the rook on f1 can capture her! `Rxf3`.
    So Black cannot capture the pawn on f3 with the Queen.

    This makes `f2f3` a very interesting move. It forces the Queen to move without allowing her to capture the pawn.

    Is there any other way Black could respond to `f2f3`?
    The Queen could move to d4, f4, e5, e3 (Wait, bishop was on e3, but it's still there), etc.

    It's attacking the Rook on f1.

    So `f2f3` also protects the Rook on f1.

    This move `f2f3` does three things:
    1.  Attacks the Black Queen on e4.
    2.  Protects the White Rook on f1.
    3.  Forces the Queen to move, which likely stops her from capturing the Knight on b1.

    This seems like a much stronger move than `e3b6`.

    Yes, `f2f3` is there.

    Black Queen on e4.
